RuDao Governance for DAOs

The DAO (Decentralized Autonomous Organization) is Web3's core organizational form. But "code is law" cannot solve every governance problem. Confucian wisdom supplies the ethical base:

VirtueCore MeaningDAO Practice
Ren 仁Protect weaker membersAnti-dilution protection; minority appeal channels
Yi 义Introduce moral constraintA "must-not-do list" on-chain; ethics committee review
Li 礼Build clear governance orderLayered governance (strategy layer vs operations layer)
Xin 信Accountable autonomyNamed executive owners per proposal; traceable responsibility

Five Principles of "Righteousness-Profit Unity" for the Token Economy

The token economy most easily falls into the "harvest the retail" trap. A RuShang token economy must follow five principles:

Tokens must be backed by real business value — not air.

Returns must match participants' actual contribution — not sudden wealth for the few.

Governance must be open and transparent — not back-room operations.

Long-termism; no pump-and-dump harvesting — not short-term pump-and-exit.

Reserve a philanthropic allocation — not everything to the team and early investors.

"Righteousness generates profit; profit benefits the people." 义以生利 利以平民

The "righteousness" of the token economy is precisely this: all participants gain fairly, rather than the few harvesting the many.

The Digital Doctrine of the Mean: Checks and Balances in DAO Governance

DAO governance needs the Middle Way:

Opposing PolesZhongYong Balance
Power vs responsibilityEqual weight: whoever decides bears the consequences
Efficiency vs democracyMajor decisions democratic; execution efficient
Technology vs humanitiesTools serve human goals; never decentralize for decentralization's sake
Innovation vs stabilityEmergency pauses; ethical-arbitration buffers

"The virtue of the mean is indeed the highest!" 中庸之为德也 其至矣乎

DAO governance is, in essence, the search for dynamic balance between code and human nature.

Confucius: "Without trust, no standing." 民无信不立

Confucius: "A person without trustworthiness — I do not know what they are good for." 人而无信 不知其可也

Inamori Kazuo: "The spirit of altruism." 利他之心

SunTzu: "Know the enemy and know yourself." 知彼知己 百战不殆

  • Before any Web3 launch, run the "integrity self-check": is the token backed by real value?
  • Write "unity of righteousness and profit" ethics clauses into the DAO charter; prohibit harvesting behavior.
  • Build on-chain data authenticity verification to keep garbage data from polluting the ecosystem.
  • In token design, reserve a philanthropic allocation — social responsibility inside the economic model.
  • Beyond the examination hall of technology lies the examination hall of civilizations. The dialogue of Toynbee and Ikeda scales the RuDao system to the measure of civilizations.
Chapter 6.2

Dialogue of Civilizations and the New Confucian Business Civilization

Toynbee, Ikeda, and the Web4 Era — Globalization 4.0

和

"History is a dialogue between civilizations."

历史是文明与文明之间的对话。

— Arnold J. Toynbee

"The twenty-first century is yearning for a 'moral ethos of symbiosis.'"

二十一世纪正渴望一个"共生的道德气质"。

— Daisaku Ikeda

"If humanity in the 21st century is to survive, it must draw wisdom from Eastern civilization, especially Chinese civilization."

21世纪的人类，如果能够生存下来，必须从东方文明尤其是中国文明中汲取智慧。

— Arnold J. Toynbee, Choose Life

As commercial civilization enters the Web4 intelligent society, we face not merely a technology upgrade but a choice at the level of civilization. The Toynbee–Ikeda dialogue gives the RuShang–RuJiang system its macro-civilizational horizon.

Toynbee's View of Civilization: Challenge and Response

The British historian Arnold Joseph Toynbee (1889–1975), in his twelve-volume A Study of History, proposed the famous theory of Challenge and Response: the rise and fall of civilizations depends on whether they can respond effectively to the challenges they face.

"Every scene in life is a problem, every turning point in growth requires effort. Only self-determined success can bring about the sublimation of vitality and the opportunity for civilizational growth."

生命的每一个场景都是问题，成长的每一个转折都需努力，唯有自决的成功，才能造成生命力的升华，带来文明成长的契机。

— Toynbee, A Study of History

"The basic cause of the rise and fall of civilizations is challenge and response. If a civilization can successfully respond to challenges, it will be born and grow."

文明兴衰的基本原因是挑战和应战。一个文明，如果能够成功地应对挑战，那么它就会诞生和成长起来。

— Toynbee, A Study of History

Placed in the contemporary business context: globalization, the AI revolution, Web3 decentralization, climate change, and the crisis of social trust are the systemic challenges facing 21st-century commercial civilization. The answer of the RuShang–RuDao system: with the complete wisdom of Confucian NeiShengWaiWang — cultivating the self and governing the enterprise within, co-opetition and win-win without — meet the challenge and complete civilization's self-renewal and the elevation of business practice.

Ikeda's Spirit of Dialogue and Philosophy of Symbiosis

Daisaku Ikeda, Buddhist thinker and educator, spent his life championing a "civilization of dialogue" and the idea of symbiosis (共生). The future of human civilization, he held, lies not in the hegemony of any single civilization but in sincere dialogue and mutual learning among many.

"Dialogue is humanity's most powerful weapon. It can break down barriers, build trust, and create peace."

对话是人类最强大的武器，它能够打破隔阂，建立信任，创造和平。

— Daisaku Ikeda

Extending symbiosis to business: the sustainability of a business ecosystem likewise depends not on one company's absolute dominance but on the symbiotic flourishing of the whole industry. The RuDao's co-opetition is precisely this philosophy of symbiosis implemented at the level of business strategy.

What the Toynbee–Ikeda Dialogue Teaches the Web4 Era

Toynbee–Ikeda ConceptRuJiang Business CorrespondenceWeb4 Significance
Challenge and responseCrisis principles: Xin first, Yi second, Li lastAI ethics crises, climate change, supply-chain restructuring are the new challenges
Dialogue of civilizationsCross-cultural cooperation; East–West fusion of business ethicsGlobalization 4.0 needs multi-civilizational co-governance
Philosophy of symbiosisCo-opetition: competition and cooperation in parallelWeb4 is the era of intelligent symbiosis — human and AI, firm and firm
Inner awakeningRuShang inner sage: cultivation; conscience-driven actionThe AI age must reassert the human being as the moral subject

Where Confucian Civilization Meets the Web4 Intelligent Society

Web4 — the fourth-generation internet — is defined as human–machine symbiosis, agent collaboration, and decentralized autonomy. In this context:

Technology needs a humanistic base: AI agents need an ethical framework — supplied by the Confucian system of Ren and Shu.

Decentralization needs accountable subjects: DAOs need answerable mechanisms — supplied by the Confucian system of Xin and Yi.

Globalization needs cultural dialogue: cross-border cooperation needs shared values — supplied by "harmony without sameness" 和而不同.

The RuDao system is, in essence, a complete business operating system for the Web4 era — one with warmth, with roots, and with strategy.

Toynbee: "History is a dialogue between civilizations." 历史是文明与文明之间的对话。

Ikeda: "The twenty-first century is yearning for a 'moral ethos of symbiosis.'" 二十一世纪正渴望一个"共生的道德气质"。

Confucius: "Within the four seas, all men are brothers." 四海之内，皆兄弟也。

Toynbee: "Humanity must draw wisdom from Eastern civilization." 必须从东方文明中汲取智慧。

Mencius: "One who has the Way will have many supporters." 得道者多助，失道者寡助。

  • Re-examine corporate strategy inside the macro frame of "civilizational challenge and response."
  • Practice the "civilization of dialogue" in cross-cultural business cooperation.
  • Move the enterprise from "zero-sum competition" toward "symbiotic ecosystems."
  • In Web4 agent design, embed the Confucian ethical framework of Ren and Shu.

Globalization 4.0 and the Predicament of Western Business Systems

Globalization has entered its 4.0 stage: competition is no longer only of products and capital, but a dialogue of civilizations and values. The New Confucian business civilization is not China's possession alone — it is Eastern wisdom's contribution to global commerce.

The Western business system created immense prosperity over two centuries — and revealed deep faults:

Short-termism: quarterly-report pressure sacrifices long-term investment.

Shareholder primacy: employees, communities, and environment marginalized.

Zero-sum competitive thinking: brittle partnerships; high cost of trust.

Alienation of capital: people become instruments; value creation yields to value extraction.

The root of these predicaments is a commercial civilization lacking the ethical core of Ren and Shu.

The Global Value of Confucian Business Thought

Confucian business thought offers another possibility:

己欲立而立人，己欲达而达人。

— The Analects · Yong Ye

The Confucian "establish others, achieve others" gives global commerce its philosophical ground for moving from zero-sum to positive-sum.

RuDao as a Global Leadership Paradigm for the 21st Century

This paradigm is not confined to the Chinese cultural sphere. NeiShengWaiWang, co-opetition, and the unity of righteousness and profit form a universal framework of business leadership:

Every culture honors integrity — Xin 信 is the cross-cultural hard currency.

Every business needs partners — Ren 仁 is the basis of cooperation.

Every market has competition — Zhi 智 and Yong 勇 are survival capabilities.

Every era needs responsibility — Yi 义 is the floor of sustainability.

Exporting More Than Goods: Exporting Eastern Business Wisdom

What China offers the world is not only manufacturing; it also includes the civilizational thinking of balanced righteousness and profit, of co-opetition and symbiosis.

"If distant people are not convinced, cultivate culture and virtue to attract them."

远人不服，则修文德以来之。

— The Analects · Ji Shi

True global influence comes not from force but from cultural virtue. The RuDao system is the contemporary practice of exactly that.

Three Characteristics of the New Confucian Business Civilization

Unity of righteousness and profit (YiLiHeYi 义利合一): profit and morality weighted together; no extremes.

Co-opetition and symbiosis (JingHeGongSheng 竞合共生): competition and cooperation in balance; ecosystems built.

Inner sage, outer king (NeiShengWaiWang 内圣外王): cultivation and achievement unified; knowledge and action one.

  • In globalization strategy, export values and business civilization, not only products.
  • Make "unity of righteousness and profit" the core value of internationalization.
  • In cross-border M&A and cooperation, practice the soft power of "cultivating virtue to attract."
  • Lead the industry from zero-sum competition to positive-sum symbiosis.
  • The system is now complete. Last, a thirty-year practitioner bears witness: does this framework hold in real business cycles?
Chapter 6.3

Case · Microsoft Saved Apple: Co-opetition and Coexistence

"The noble person seeks harmony, not conformity." 君子和而不同 — Confucius

和

"The noble person seeks harmony, not conformity."

君子和而不同。

— Confucius 孔子

The Story

In 1997, Apple was on the brink of bankruptcy. On August 6, at Macworld Boston, Steve Jobs brought out an unexpected guest — Bill Gates, appearing via satellite video. Microsoft announced it would invest $150 million in non-voting Apple shares and commit to continuing Office for the Mac.

This "rival-saving-rival" move is widely credited with saving Apple. Years later, as Apple's stock soared, Microsoft's stake was worth billions.

Why Co-opetition, Not Kill-or-Be-Killed

A zero-sum reading would have said: let Apple die, one less rival. But that short-term "victory" would have cost Microsoft dearly — antitrust scrutiny, public backlash, a shrinking Office market, and an ecosystem in retreat.

Co-opetition read the long-term account instead. By keeping Apple alive, Microsoft avoided the stigma of monopoly, protected Office's place on every platform, and let two ecosystems grow together. It conceded in the short term to win together in the long term. The pie did not shrink; it grew.

The RuDao Reading

Three principles of RuDao explain what Microsoft did.

First, harmony without uniformity. The two companies did not become the same; each kept its own ecosystem and its own strengths, coexisting through difference rather than trying to absorb the other.

Second, unity of righteousness and profit. Microsoft's righteousness — saving the industry, honoring its commitments, avoiding the reputation of a monopoly — and its profit — keeping Office relevant, earning goodwill, and reaping later returns — were not in conflict. They converged.

Third, standing on trust. A single investment and pledge became a trust baseline between two giants, paving the way for years of cooperation across many fields.

Lessons for IT & Software Industry Ecosystem

In the AI and blockchain era, ecosystems are worth more than zero-sum victories. A rival is often the best partner — and the most valuable potential ally. Microsoft did not save Apple out of charity; it calculated the long-term account the RuDao way, through harmony and difference.

The true winner does not make its rival disappear; it makes its rival the mirror that makes itself better.

案例｜微软救苹果：竞合与共存

"君子和而不同。"

一、故事

1997年，苹果濒临破产。8月6日，波士顿 Macworld 大会上，乔布斯请出一位"不速之客"——比尔·盖茨，通过卫星视频亮相。微软当场宣布，投资1.5亿美元购买苹果无投票权股票，并承诺继续为 Mac 开发 Office 软件。

这一"宿敌救宿敌"之举，被视为挽救苹果的关键一役。几年后苹果股价暴涨，微软这笔投资市值一度高达数十亿美元。

二、为什么是"竞合"而非"你死我活"

零和思维会这样算账：让苹果死，少一个对手。但这份"短期的胜利"会让微软付出惨重代价——反垄断审查、公众指责、Office 失去 Mac 市场，以及整个生态的收缩。

竞合之道算的是另一笔长账。救活苹果，微软避开了垄断之讥，保住了 Office 在全平台的地位，也让两大生态一起做大。它短期让利，长期共赢；蛋糕没有变小，反而变大了。

三、儒道的解读

儒道的三条原则，恰好解释了微软的选择。

第一，和而不同。两家并没有变得一样，而是各守生态、各擅其长，在差异中共生，而不是试图吞并对方。

第二，义利合一。微软的"义"——挽救行业、信守承诺、避免垄断恶名——和它的"利"——保住 Office、赢得美誉、收获日后回报——并不冲突，反而合一。

第三，以信立身。一纸投资与承诺，成了两大巨头之间多年的"信任基线"，也为日后在许多领域的合作铺平了道路。

四、给数字商业的启示

在人工智能与区块链的时代，生态比零和更值钱。对手往往是最好的伙伴，也是最大的潜在盟友。微软救苹果，不是慈善，而是用儒道式的"和而不同"算清了那笔长账。

真正的赢家，不是让对手消失，而是让对手成为让自己更好的那面镜子。

Chapter 6.4

A Thirty-Year Practitioner's Witness: From Web1.0 Through AI and Web3 to Web4

Unity of Knowledge and Action 知行合一 — The RuJiang's Path Advances Daily

行

"At fifteen, I resolved to pursue learning. At thirty, I established my principles. At forty, I had no doubts. At fifty, I understood the mandate of heaven. At sixty, I accepted all words with ease. At seventy, I followed my heart's desire without transgressing norms."

吾十有五而志于学，三十而立，四十而不惑，五十而知天命，六十而耳顺，七十而从心所欲，不逾矩。

— The Analects · Wei Zheng

"To know but not to act is still not to know."

知而不行，只是未知。

— Wang Yangming

"Let even your enemies believe in you."

让你的敌人都相信你。

— Li Ka-shing

This chapter offers the author's own practice as a living proof of "unity of knowledge and action" (ZhiXingHeYi 知行合一). Thirty years of practice — from Web1.0 to Web4. The technology kept changing; the core of RuShang and RuJiang never did.

School Days at King's College, Hong Kong: The Seed Is Planted

King's College, Hong Kong, is the alma mater of Professor Lau Din-cheuk 劉殿爵, the authoritative English translator of the Analects. As a junior alumnus of Professor Lau, I encountered Confucian culture from youth. Professor Lau "translated" Confucius from the Chinese context into the world's context — that spirit of cultural translation shaped my whole life. The RuShang seed: commerce is not only exchange of goods — it is the transmission of values.

The Web1.0 Era: The Trial of a Nasdaq Listing

In 2000, I co-founded Pacificnet, which listed on Nasdaq. In that dot-com bubble era, many companies attracted investment with hype and fabricated numbers. We insisted on real transactions, real technology patents, long-term value.

The lesson: holding the bottom line in a bubble matters more than chasing profit in a boom. "Wealth and rank attained through unrighteousness are to me like drifting clouds." 不义而富且贵，于我如浮云。

The Web2.0 Era: The Discipline of Listed-Company Governance

Afterward I served as independent director of several listed companies, converting the righteousness-profit discernment into governance principles. Across cycles of volatility, integrity and compliance proved the best protection.

The lesson: the core of corporate governance is not the institutions themselves but the values behind them. "A person without trustworthiness — I do not know what they are good for." 人而无信，不知其可也。

The Web3 and AI Era: Advocating the RuShang-Type Blockchain Entrepreneur

As co-founder of the Hong Kong Blockchain Association, I promoted the ideal of the "RuShang-type blockchain entrepreneur." In universities I taught AI ethics, using Confucian wisdom to "translate" technological problems.

The lesson: the best algorithm is not the fastest one, but the one that best understands fairness and benevolence. The blockchain can record transactions — it cannot record the human conscience. "Without trust, no standing." 民无信不立。

Thirty Years, One Summary

EraTechnological CharacterRuShang–RuJiang Application

Web2.0Social, platformsCorporate governance; compliance
Web3Blockchain, DAOToken ethics; decentralized governance

A Closing Word: The RuJiang's Path Advances Daily

"Building a mountain: if one basket short of completion, stopping — I stop. Filling a level ground: even if only one basket poured, going forward — I go forward."

譬如为山，未成一篑，止，吾止也。譬如平地，虽覆一篑，进，吾往也。

— The Analects · Zi Han

The RuShang is the inner discipline; the RuJiang, the outer practice. The RuJiang's path does not depend on a perfect start — it depends on advancing every day. Technologies iterate and renew, but the essence of business remains the exchange of trust and value between people — and the Confucian wisdom of 2,500 years still carries real power today.

Confucius: "At fifteen, I resolved to pursue learning..." 吾十有五而志于学……

Wang Yangming: "To know but not to act is still not to know." 知而不行 只是未知。

Li Ka-shing: "Let even your enemies believe in you." 让你的敌人都相信你。

Confucius: "Building a mountain, one basket short — stopping, I stop." 譬如为山，未成一篑，止，吾止也。

  • Write your personal and corporate timeline; mark the tests of values at each key choice.
  • Reflect: amid technological change, which business essentials never change?
  • Draft a ten-year "RuJiang cultivation plan," landing Inner Sage and Outer King in yearly actions.
  • Build your own habit of daily advance: one basketful each day — never stop.
Chapter 6.5

Synthesis: 以德驭术 as the Operating System of the Digital Age

Governing Technique with Virtue — Confucian Virtue Ethics and AI Business Governance

德

Confucian Virtue Ethics and AI Business Governance

"Do not impose on others what you do not wish for yourself."

己所不欲，勿施于人。

— The Analects · Wei Ling Gong

"Do not worry about scarcity, but about inequality; do not worry about poverty, but about instability."

不患寡而患不均，不患贫而患不安。

— The Analects · Ji Shi

"A gentleman understands righteousness; a petty person understands only profit."

君子喻于义，小人喻于利。

— The Analects · Li Ren

"Without compass and square, one cannot draw circles and squares."

不以规矩，不能成方圆。

— Mencius · Li Lou I

AI is not a value-neutral instrument. Behind every algorithm stands a human choice; behind every dataset stands a human bias. When AI enters the core of business — hiring, credit, medicine, marketing, risk control — ethics is no longer an option; it is the floor of survival.

Four Real Pain Points of Commercial AI

Algorithmic bias. Training data carries humanity's historical prejudice, producing unfair outcomes in hiring, credit, marketing, and risk control. From the Confucian Way of Ren and Shu, every person holds dignity and deserves fair treatment. "Do not impose on others what you do not wish for yourself" — if you would not be discriminated against for gender, age, or region, do not let AI discriminate against others.

Profit-only optimization. Most commercial AI optimizes solely for short-term revenue, conversion, and clicks — replicating the ordinary merchant's "profit first, righteousness after," amplified exponentially by the algorithm. "The gentleman understands righteousness; the petty person understands only profit." An algorithm that maximizes profit alone becomes the "petty person" of the digital age.

Missing humanity; floating accountability. When AI decisions go wrong, firms often deflect: "the algorithm's fault." Confucian Xin requires traceable responsibility and kept promises. "A person without trustworthiness — I do not know what they are good for." If AI can escape accountability, the enterprise credit system collapses.

The black-box moral vacuum. Large-model reasoning cannot be audited; in critical business decisions this creates a moral vacuum. "Without compass and square, one cannot draw circles and squares." AI without rules and audit is a compass-less drawing — disorder must follow.

The Confucian Virtue-Ethics Framework for AI

VirtueAI Ethics CorrespondenceCore Requirement
Ren 仁Human-centered AIServe human flourishing, not only corporate KPIs
Yi 义Algorithmic justiceNo unjust profit-seeking; no exploitative business models
Shu 恕The empathy testVerify algorithmic fairness from each stakeholder's perspective
Xin 信Accountable AIHumans bear the final responsibility

The Doctrine of the Mean: Constraining Single-Objective Optimization

AI design should abandon "single-objective maximization" for "multi-objective dynamic balance":

BalanceOpposing PolesZhongYong Strategy
Profit vs fairnessShareholder return vs user rightsSet "fairness constraints"
Efficiency vs humanityAutomation vs human experienceKeep a "human-intervention interface"
Innovation vs riskBreakthrough vs safety floorAdopt "progressive deployment"
Personalization vs privacyPrecise service vs data protection"Minimum-necessary data" principle

"The virtue of the mean is indeed the highest!"

中庸之为德也，其至矣乎！

— The Analects · Yong Ye

The essence of AI ethics is the Doctrine of the Mean for the technological age: impartiality, dynamic balance.

The Five-Layer RuShang AI Decision Model (in Practice)

LayerNameCore FunctionResponsible
L1Value-requirements layerFix moral constraints before model trainingCEO / Board
L2Data-labeling layerFilter biased data; flag unethical samplesAI Ethics Committee
L3Objective-function layerAdd fairness and benevolence constraints to profit objectivesAlgorithm engineers
L4Human-in-the-loop layerHuman final review for high-risk AI decisionsBusiness owners
L5Continuous-audit layerPeriodic post-launch verification against moral driftIndependent third-party audit

Closed loop: L1 → L2 → L3 → L4 → L5 → feedback to L1, iterating continuously.

Technical illustration:

Traditional model: maximize profit = Max(Revenue − Cost)

RuShang model: Max(Revenue − Cost − α × Bias_Score − β × Harm_Score)

where α and β are moral-constraint weights set by the board.

East–West Complementarity in AI Ethics

DimensionMainstream Western AI EthicsConfucian AI Ethics (this framework)
Philosophical rootIndividual rights (privacy, liberty)Relational ethics (Ren, Yi, Shu, Xin)
Core question"Does the technology violate individual rights?""Does the technology foster human harmony?"
MethodRule-based (compliance checklists)Virtue-based (human cultivation + institutional design)
Accountability"Who broke the law?""Who ultimately answers for the result?"

Core conclusion: the two are not opposites but complements. The Western framework supplies clear rules and processes; the Confucian framework supplies deep humanistic care and cultural self-awareness. The best AI governance is "Western rule frameworks + Eastern moral core."

Confucius: "Do not impose on others what you do not wish for yourself." 己所不欲，勿施于人。

Confucius: "Worry not about scarcity but about inequality." 不患寡而患不均。

Mencius: "Without compass and square, one cannot draw circles and squares." 不以规矩，不能成方圆。

Wang Yangming: "To know good and evil is conscience." 知善知恶是良知。

  • Build a corporate AI-ethics self-audit covering bias, data privacy, and accountability.
  • Establish an AI ethics committee with veto power.
  • Draft the "AI must-not-do list": scenarios where fully autonomous AI decisions are prohibited.
  • Mandate human-in-the-loop review in all high-risk AI scenarios.
  • Run regular "empathy tests": examine algorithmic fairness from the most vulnerable stakeholder's perspective.
  • AI needs an ethical base; so does Web3. The blockchain can record everything — but it cannot guarantee the truth of what is entered.

Co-authored by Qin Zhong and Chen Yanru, it was published by Guangxi Ethnic Publishing House in 1995.《从海武田飞出的歌》是记述香港实业家唐楚三的传记文学作品，由秦忠、陈妍如合著，广西民族出版社 1995 年出版。

The Classic of WeiQi/Go (Qi Jing Thirteen Chapters) 《棋经十三篇·论局篇》名言出处

经典出处与原文字源 (The Origin)

Author: Zhang Ni 张拟 · Scholar of the Hanlin Academy 翰林学士 · Song Dynasty 宋 (c. 1049-1054 CE)

《儒道》章节「棋品见人品 博弈礼仪 — 在危机与逆境中生存的儒将智慧」

English

"As stated in the Classic of WeiQi/Go (Qi Jing): 'Those who excel at winning do not descend into petty contention; those who excel at facing defeat do not lose their internal order.' By refraining from petty contention, one's strategic depth remains unfathomable; by maintaining order in defeat, one's resilience remains unassailable."

中文原文与阐释

《棋经十三篇·论局篇》有言:「善胜者不争，善败者不乱。」不争，则深不可测；不乱，则坚不可犯。胜者不逞一时之勇，败者不失君子之节，这不仅是弈棋的礼仪，更是穿越商业周期的无上心法。

《棋经》名言出处

出自北宋皇祐年间（约 1049-1054 年）由翰林学士张拟所著的《棋经十三篇》，这是中国历史上第一部系统性的围棋理论著作，地位堪称围棋界的《孙子兵法》。

《棋经十三篇·论局篇第一》原文为:

「博弈之道，贵乎谨严。……胜不言放，败不言躁。其用战也，当如兵之法。故善胜者不争，善败者不乱。不争，则深不可测；不乱，则坚不可犯。」

核心含义阐释

  • 善胜者不争:真正擅长制胜的弈者，不会去争一日之长、一时之气，而是布局宏大、运筹帷幄，让对手不战而自溃（呼应孙子「不战而屈人之兵」与儒家「和而不同」）。
  • 善败者不乱:真正懂得面对失败的人，在身处逆境、棋局溃败时，内心绝不慌乱，而是坚守阵脚、沉着寻找生机。
7.5

HuaXue 华学 — the Confucian Academy and modern RuShang education

HuaXue-EnXue 华学恩学 · Class Insights 课程心得

HuaXue EnXue (华学恩学), founded by Professor Xiao Xianhua (肖先华 / 萧先华), is a contemporary system of teachings that blends traditional Chinese philosophies — primarily Confucianism, Song-Ming Neo-Confucianism, Taoism, and Buddhism — into practical principles for modern life, family dynamics, and business management.

HuaXue-EnXue 华学恩学 is a modern Confucian education system founded by Professor Xiao Xianhua 肖先华. Its core system: "One Dao, Two Roots, Three Hearts, Four Refrainings, Five Questions, Six Harmonies, Seven Essentials, Eight Discernments, Nine Emptinesses, Ten Virtues, Twenty Methods" resonates deeply with Tony Tong's RuDao framework, forming a complete modern practice system for the Confucian merchant.

Key mottos, principles, and concepts HuaXue by Professor Xiao Xianhua within his lectures and volunteer training guidelines include:

1. The Core Pillar Core Values: three central attitudes:

"With a heart of gratitude, a heart of respect, and a heart for the public good. " "以感恩心、以恭敬心、以天下为公心"

Prof Xiao instills an active attitude of selflessness and humility:

"Only show gratitude, never complain; only show respect, never be arrogant, and let go of the ego. "

"只感恩，不抱怨；只恭敬，不傲慢，不我执。"

"Treat people well, only give gratitude, and do not hold grievances. "

"对人好，只感恩不抱怨。"

"Stand high upon the mountain peak, but walk deeply along the ocean floor — meaning to maintain noble aspirations while executing with absolute humility. "

"高高山顶立，低低海底行。"

"Only when your heart is dedicated to the public good can true success be achieved. "

"天下为公，才能成功。"

Dr. Tong Yan Kai ( Tang EnJia湯恩佳博士 香港孔教学院院长 世界儒商联合会会长), former industrialist and President of the Confucian Academy ( ConfucianAcademy.com ) in Hong Kong, is one of the most important modern advocates for RuShang ethics. His famous definition distinguishes the RuShang from ordinary merchants: "The greatest difference between a RuShang and an ordinary merchant is that the RuShang attaches great importance to business morality and refuses wealth obtained through unrighteous means."

Dr. Tong advocates the Confucian Ten Virtues (十德): rén 仁，yì 义，lǐ 礼，zhì 智，xìn 信，xiào 孝，tì 悌，zhōng 忠，lián 廉，chǐ 耻. This ten-virtue framework forms the direct foundation for the modern Eight Virtues of the RuShang (儒商八德). Dr. Tong stresses that wealth is not an end in itself. He holds the principle of shēng cái yǒu dào 生财有道 (create wealth in the proper way), and adopts Confucius's maxim "Wealth and honour obtained unrighteously are to me like floating clouds" as his personal motto.

For Dr. Tong, the RuShang pursues profit, but profit must serve benevolent purposes. This idea of yǐ cái fā shēn 以财发身 — using wealth to cultivate moral character aligns perfectly with the RuDao thesis: Confucian virtue forms the unchanging substance (体，tǐ) of enterprise, while adaptive business strategy constitutes the function (用，yòng). Dr. Tong describes this model as "The Analects plus the abacus", combining Confucian moral cultivation with practical commercial operation.

Dr. Tong said: "To be a RuShang is to uphold commercial morality and reject ill-gotten gains. Wealth acquired rightly is a means to carry out benevolent works. Wealth exists to cultivate one's moral character: we use wealth to develop benevolence."

"儒商就是要重视商业道德，不取不义之财，生财要取之有道，仁者生财是用来发展仁的事业，仁是儒家思想的核心，财是实现仁的事业的手段，故曰 以财发身"。

Source: Dr. Tong's Lecture at Sichuan University 四川大学 2005

Keywords of Dr. Tong: yǐ cái fā shēn 以财发身，righteous wealth, benevolence as purpose, wealth as instrument.

The Core Thought System of HuaXue-EnXue

HuaXue-EnXue carries on the orthodox current of Chinese culture, summarizing and developing Confucianism, Daoism, and Buddhism. Its core mission:

"To build, for the Chinese nation and indeed for all humanity, a system of thought and value by which to stand and live between heaven and earth. HuaXue-EnXue is the distillation of the finest elements of the world's cultures and religions — the village Way, village learning, and village rules of the global village; the family Way, family learning, and family rules of humanity's common earthly home."

The core system and its correspondence to this book:

SystemContentRuShang–RuJiang Correspondence
One Dao 一道The great Way of the universe and human lifeThe Way of the RuShang — unity of righteousness and profit
Two Roots 二根Loyalty and filial piety fulfilled togetherThe RuShang root — nation-and-society accountability
Three Hearts 三心Gratitude, reverence, responsibilityThe RuJiang's three virtues — gratitude, humility, ownership
Four Refrainings 四戒No arrogance, no complaining, no greed, no slacknessThe RuShang's four refrainings — the base of self-cultivation
Five Questions 五问Daily self-examination in five questionsThe RuJiang's five questions — pre-decision self-check
Six Harmonies 六和Body–mind, family, enterprise, society, the world, heaven-and-earthCo-opetition's six harmonies — symbiotic win-win
Seven Essentials 七要Seven core essentialsThe RuJiang's seven essentials — strategic capability
Eight Discernments 八辨Eight criteria for judging right and wrongThe Eight Virtues — the ethical floor
Nine Emptinesses 九无Nine states beyond attachmentThe Inner Sage's nine emptinesses — the selfless realm
Ten Virtues 十德Ten core virtuesThe RuShang's ten virtues — the complete personality
Twenty Methods 二十法Twenty practicesThe RuJiang's twenty methods — the action guide

The Core Ideas Explained

(1) Awaken to the Dao; root yourself; connect to the root.

"Devoutly awaken to the Dao; go deep in one discipline and take root — root in loyalty and filial piety fulfilled together; find the root of Chinese culture, the root of one's life and career. Solve the relation of Dao and technique from the root upward. Much past training only studied technique; now strive for the Dao. Only by learning, awakening, clarifying, and walking the Dao can technique finally be handled rightly, and can life and work return to their proper orbit."

RuShang reading: the inner-sage foundation — awaken to the Dao first, study technique second; cultivate the self first, manage the enterprise second.

(2) Before doing, learn to be human.

Professor Xiao stresses: "Before doing, learn to be human. Face life with a healthy mindset; find the root of Chinese culture and of one's life and career... Learn and carry forward the finest of Chinese culture; revive national education and national culture."

RuShang reading: the RuShang's core logic — self-cultivation as the root, enterprise management as the branch.

(3) Only reverence; no arrogance; no ego-attachment.

"Only reverence — no arrogance, no ego-attachment. Serve others; do not cling; do not overstep your station; do not promise lightly; do not take advantage; do not covet others' money or goods; feel neither aggrieved nor wounded."

RuShang reading: the RuShang's stance toward partners — humble, sincere, never overstepping, never grasping.

(4) Filial piety first among a hundred virtues.

"Filial piety is foremost of the hundred virtues. Be grateful to parents; seek their grace at the wellspring of life; teaching filial piety is the golden key that solves all problems. One must first achieve filial piety... Be grateful to every person who appears in your life; be good to people; only gratitude, no complaining; only reverence."

RuShang reading: the starting point of nation-and-society feeling — filial piety is the wellspring of gratitude, and gratitude the starting point of trust.

(5) Return to the scholar-merchant's devotion to family and nation.

"HuaXue-EnXue is devoted to carrying forward the finest of Chinese culture, bridging Chinese and Western management wisdom, transmitting the mission of enduring foundations, and returning to the scholar-merchant's devotion to family and nation — reconnecting to the living source of five thousand years of Huaxia culture... cultivating people of resolve who can love and serve the country and give their strength to the nation's rejuvenation and the sustained, healthy development of its economy."

RuShang reading: the ultimate mission — scholar and merchant as one; accountability to family and nation.

Principal Zhang Haibiao's Scholar-Merchant Theory 士商理论

At the ninth anniversary celebration of International HuaXue-EnXue (2020), themed "Cultivate deeply for nine years with goodwill; sprint for ten years to gain enlightenment," HuaXue family members from Italy, Spain, the United States, France, Australia, China, and beyond joined — nearly a thousand participants. Zhang Haibiao (Bill Zhang), head of the International HuaXue-EnXue Business School, has dedicated himself to bringing HuaXue-EnXue into business education.

The Business School's Scholar-Merchant Theory (ShiShangLiLun 士商理论) is precisely the modern expression of "RuShang and RuJiang": scholar-spirit, merchant-competence (ShiHunShangCai 士魂商才); Inner Sage, Outer King. It fuses the moral cultivation of the Confucian scholar with the commercial competence of the modern merchant — in full accord with this book's framework of "RuShang as Inner Sage, RuJiang as Outer King."

Principal Zhang Haibiao's Scholar-Merchant Theory

"Success is: each keeps to their proper role and fulfills their duty."

成功就是: 各守本分，完成本职。

— Professor Xiao Xianhua

"Cultivate deeply for nine years with goodwill; sprint for ten years to gain enlightenment."

深耕九载对你好，冲刺十年大开窍。

— Principal Zhang Haibiao

"Filial piety is the foremost of all virtues."

百善孝为先。

HuaXue-EnXue 华学恩学 · Class Insights 课程心得

HuaXue-EnXue 华学恩学 Class Insights

Chinese Key Words: 诗情画意、琴棋书画、涵养心性、礼乐文脉润人生
Author: HKBA Tony Tong (唐仪), Student of HuaXue-357 Class
Date & Location: May 1-6, 2026, Shenzhen, China

My teacher, Master Xiao Xianhua (肖先华 / 萧先华), is the 74th-generation descendant of Xiao He (萧何), the famous Prime Minister (相国) of the Han Dynasty (汉朝) under the founding Emperor Liu Bang (刘邦).

I was fortunate to attend a HuaXue lecture delivered by Master Teacher Xiao Xianhua 肖先华, an enlightening session that brought me thorough clarity and profound insights. Master Xiao earnestly taught that one should live with poetic and pictorial grace (诗情画意) and abide by the principles of ritual and music (礼乐).

华学上课心得:诗情画意 琴棋书画 涵养心性 礼乐文脉润人生

作者:唐仪 TonyTong.cc · RuDao.org
HuaXue-EnXue 华学357期学员
2026年5月1-6日 于深圳

今日有幸在华学课堂聆听肖先华老师传道授业，心中豁然开朗，收获满满。老师谆谆教诲，为人处世当心怀诗情画意，更要恪守礼乐之道，懂乐明礼、修身养性，方能成就君子品格。课堂上，老师细数中华历代圣人先贤，谈及孔子、诸葛亮、周瑜皆为抚琴高手，以琴修心、以乐立身的史实，更让我对琴棋书画、礼乐文脉有了前所未有的深刻感悟。作为一名学习钢琴、小提琴、笛子多种乐器的爱好者，我更能体会到乐礼相融的文化底蕴，读懂中华传统文化的深厚魅力。

中华五千年文明绵延不绝，礼与乐始终是文化核心、立身之本。礼定秩序、规言行，让我们为人处世有尺有度；乐润心灵、养性情，让我们内心平和澄澈。而琴，自古便是乐之精髓，是文人圣贤、仁人君子不可或缺的修行载体，琴棋书画合称"文人四艺"，更是刻在中国人血脉里的修身法门。肖老师课堂上提及的古圣先贤精通琴艺，并非文学演绎，而是有正史典籍明确记载的史实，每一段故事都印证着"以琴修心、以乐载道"的文化传统。

至圣先师孔子，是中华礼乐文化的奠基者，更是将琴艺与修身融为一体的圣人。据《史记·孔子世家》《孔子家语》等正史记载，孔子曾拜鲁国乐官师襄子学琴，研习《文王操》时，他不满足于熟练弹奏曲调、掌握技法，而是潜心体悟琴中志向，直至在琴音中感知文王的圣贤人格、领悟仁德精髓才肯作罢。周游列国期间，孔子身陷陈蔡绝粮的困境，却依旧讲诵弦歌不衰，以琴乐安定弟子心神，坚守心中道义与理想。他提出"兴于诗，立于礼，成于乐"，将琴乐纳入"六艺"教育体系，在孔子心中，弹琴从来不是单纯的技艺消遣，而是教化世人、涵养仁德、修身立德的根本途径。

三国名相诸葛亮，字孔明，亦是精通琴道、以琴明志的先贤典范。正史《三国志·诸葛亮传》明确记载，诸葛亮躬耕隆中时，好为《梁父吟》，常抚琴吟唱，琴声中寄托着心系天下、济世安民的远大抱负；《襄阳耆旧记》亦有言，孔明常登高鼓瑟抚琴，寄情于乐；《中兴书目》更记载其著《琴经》一卷，详述制琴渊源与七弦乐理，是名副其实的音律大家。而《三国演义》中空城计焚香操琴的桥段，虽为文学演绎，却将诸葛亮以琴静心、临危不乱的气度刻画得淋漓尽致，这份从容智慧，正是常年抚琴修身、沉淀心性的成果。

东吴儒将周瑜，其琴艺造诣更是有正史铁证。《三国志·吴书·周瑜传》原文记载:"瑜少精意于音乐，虽三爵之后，其有阙误，瑜必知之，知之必顾。故时人谣曰:'曲有误，周郎顾。'"即便饮酒微醉，旁人弹琴稍有音误，他都能立刻察觉，足见其对音律、琴艺的精通程度。身为沙场将帅，周瑜以琴艺涵养儒雅风骨，兼具武将的果敢与文人的雅致，完美诠释了古人文武兼备、礼乐修身的君子风范。

三位先贤抚琴修身的史实，让我深刻明白:琴棋书画，艺艺皆修心。琴为心声，可涤荡浮躁、沉静内心；棋为智慧，可锤炼格局、思虑周全；书为心画，可端正品行、沉稳心性；画为意境，可感知天地、涵养诗情。而礼乐相伴，更是让这份修行有了根基，懂礼则言行方正，知乐则心境平和，这正是中华传统文化教给我们的做人之道。

平日里，我坚持学习钢琴、小提琴、笛子，在器乐演奏中感受音律之美，却未曾深入探寻背后的礼乐文脉与修身真谛。此次华学课堂，经肖老师点拨，才真正懂得，乐器演奏不只是技法的锤炼，更是传承礼乐文化、涵养自身心性的过程。当下社会节奏飞快，人心易浮躁，而琴棋书画与礼乐文化，正是让我们沉淀内心、回归本真的良方。

今后，我将牢记肖先华老师的教诲，以古圣先贤为榜样，深耕中华优秀传统文化，在琴棋书画中涵养心性，在礼乐文脉中砥砺品行。心怀诗情画意，恪守有礼有乐，既要精进乐器技艺，更要传承文化精髓，做礼乐文脉的践行者、华学精神的传承者，在传统文化的滋养中，修身立德、行稳致远。

感谢华学-恩学的肖先华老师，承蒙老师讲授华学，令我体悟礼乐与琴道的修身法门，启发本书儒道一体的核心思考。

同时感恩张海标校长，在儒家思想、儒商精神与中华传统文化层面，给予我关于"义利合一、竞合模式"的深刻启发。

唐仪 华学357期学员 · 2026年5月1-6日 于深圳 · TonyTong.cc · RuDao.org

7.6

Tong Legacy and A Father's PrideAfterword to RuDao ·
唐氏传奇-父亲的骄傲:《儒道》后记

大唐威仪 唐家骄傲 — Inscribed by his father, Tong Cho Sam 唐楚三

Tony Tong (唐仪) in Chinese is "Tang-Yi". Tang or Tong is our family name, and "Yi" 仪/义 means Righteousness/Integrity. As the ancient sages once observed: "If names are not corrected, language will not be fluent; if language is not fluent, affairs cannot be successfully completed." Reflecting upon his half-century of conduct, the character "Yi" (仪 — Stature/Law/Majesty) in his name has served as a profound spiritual anchor across the grand rivers of his life journey. It embodies the majestic stature of the Great Tang Dynasty and stands as the pride of the Tong family lineage.

I. Sacred Ancestry and the Golden Era of Tong/Tang

Since antiquity, the Tong family has carried a baseline genetic code of profound strategy, grand wisdom, and statecraft. Tracing back to the absolute dawn of Chinese civilization, the roots of the Tong lineage extend directly to the golden era of Sage Kings Yao and Shun. The ancient sovereign Emperor Yao was originally enfeoffed in the lands of Tang, establishing the legendary "Tao-Tang" epoch celebrated across thousands of generations. His descendants adopted the name of the realm as their surname, forging one of the oldest and most revered family lines in Chinese history. From the benevolent abdication policies of Emperor Yao to the Warring States diplomat Zhang Yi—who neutralized entire enemy states through sheer strategic alliance without spilling a single drop of blood—this bloodline totem of prioritizing grand strategy over blunt force has long been etched into our family ethos.

Centuries later, this legacy culminated in the majestic aura of the Great Tang Dynasty (大唐威仪), which commanded structural peace under heaven. The supreme power of the Tang Empire lay not in military conquest, but in its unparalleled cultural inclusivity, global universalism, and its magnificent framework of ritual, music, and benevolent kingly governance (王道).

In modern history, Tang Shaoyi (唐绍仪), the very first Prime Minister of the Republic of China, was born in 1862 right along the shores of the South China Sea in Tangjiawan, Zhuhai. Today, this exact region stands as the absolute vital core of the Greater Bay Area (GBA)—the launching pad of China's contemporary economic miracle and technological rise. Standing tall within this ancient town, the historic "Tangjia Three Temples" serve as a silent monument to our clan's prosperous heritage of "the righteous gentleman who delights in practicing virtue." It bears the lineage's generational wisdom of leadership and clan dignity. When Prime Minister Tang Shaoyi served as the magistrate of Zhongshan, he famously built his mountain estate and unconditionally donated his private imperial garden, "Gongle Park," to the public. This selfless act of enriching the community and pursuing win-win alliances represents the purest manifestation of a true Confucian "Benevolent Leader" (仁者) and the ancestral practice of "Coexistence of Righteousness and Profit." As a son of the Tong family, Tony Tong naturally inherited this grand, inclusive capacity for strategic statecraft and regional brilliance.

II. The Blood of Chu: The Fighting Spirit of the Tong Clan

An exceptional son is forged under the guidance of a resolute father. My name is Tong Cho Sam (唐楚三), a name that carries the fiercest and most unbreakable backbone of Chinese history: "Though Chu be reduced to a mere three households, the ultimate downfall of Qin must belong to Chu!" (楚虽三户，亡秦必楚).

This earth-shattering historical maxim originates from Sima Qian's Records of the Grand Historian (Shiji史记: Biographies of Xiang Yu). Originally proclaimed by Lord Nan of the ancient State of Chu during an era of total foreign invasion and fractured borders, this prophecy encapsulates the ultimate Fighting Spirit of our people—an unyielding, iron-blooded resilience to protect the homeland and resist outside adversity. It dictates that even when pushed into the absolute dark of a crisis, even if only three households remain, a family of true character will never bow their heads to external invaders; their absolute determination will inevitably shatter tyrannical power!

This indomitable fighting spirit is stamped into the very bone of the Tong clan. It guided my own lifelong journey as an electrical-mechanical engineer and inventor, navigating the turbulent tides of history to contribute to national infrastructure. Inevitably, this same spirit passed into Tony's DNA. It is precisely because of this relentless Tong family fighting spirit—this courage to directly confront storms, mitigate external crises, and defend principles without giving up an inch—that he possesses that core, bulletproof resilience to remain unshakeable in the face of crisis and discover breakthrough lifelines across the volatile battlegrounds of global technology.

III. The Harmony of Ritual and Strategy

Nurtured by this balanced family discipline of martial grit and scholarly refinement, every milestone of Tony's growth has mirrored the noble "Yi" (仪) of a true gentleman. He was trained in the scholarly arts of music, chess, calligraphy, and painting from the age of five. By age nine, he stood as the sole representative of his school, performing a masterful violin solo at the city-wide arts festival to immense acclaim. He went on to earn admission into Hong Kong's premier institution, King's College, and joined the Hong Kong Youth Symphony Orchestra, performing frequently on major stages. For his higher education, he crossed the globe to major in engineering at the University of Minnesota, USA.

This path was a seamless fusion of structural etiquette and cultural depth. The mastery of the scholarly arts did not merely sharpen his strategic vision; it forged his inner character, granting him the balanced foundation of a true leader: an inner sage with outer competence (内圣外王).

IV. The Architecture of Trust: Etiquette and Righteousness

In the Chinese language, "Yi" (仪 — Stature/Law) and "Yi" (义 — Righteousness/Integrity) share the exact same phonetic root and origin, converging fundamentally into the foundational operating system of Chinese governance: "Li-Yi" (礼仪 — Ritual and Etiquette).

Tracing the etymology to its earliest origins, the traditional character for Stature, "儀", is composed of "Man" (人) on the left and "Righteousness" (義) on the right. In Oracle Bone Script and Bronze Inscriptions, the right side originally depicted a majestic weapon held by a warrior adorned in ceremonial regalia—a valiant protector standing tall to defend justice and fairness. The precise posture, sacred laws, and dignified presence demonstrated by this protector during grand state ceremonies form the true linguistic origin of "Yi." Without Righteousness, Stature cannot exist.

Furthermore, "Yi" has been intrinsically linked with "Li" (礼 — Ritual/Propriety) since the dawn of time. In Oracle Bone Script, the character "禮" depicts precious jade stones placed neatly inside a sacred bronze ritual vessel (豆). Its original meaning represents the act of reverently offering up the finest treasures of the earth to express ultimate respect to the cosmos and the ancestors. In Chinese civilization, "Li" is the supreme cosmic law that governs human relationships, representing institutional order, mutual respect, and contractual consensus. "Yi" is the elegant, visible execution of that sacred law in the human world. Etiquette without morality is hollow; stature without character is superficial. When unified, they form the highest trust architecture for human conduct and global enterprise.

While "Li-Yi" represents the visible order of civilization, "Yi" (义) represents the moral execution of justice. Across his three decades of cross-border entrepreneurship—spanning four distinct waves of the high-tech revolution—Tony Tong masterfully distilled this ancestral wisdom into the operational core of modern commerce: The Coexistence of Righteousness and Profit (义利共存), and Harmony in Difference (和而不同).

While the world mistakenly views the marketplace as a brutal, zero-sum battlefield, Tony wields the sword of integrity to cut through the cold algorithms of the digital century. In this frontier era of Web3, AI, and borderless digital finance, his lifework proves that a true gentleman never sacrifices human relationship for short-term margin. Instead, he governs commercial workflows with pristine etiquette and secures long-term strategic alliances through unshakeable trust. As Confucius stated: "When seeing gain, think of righteousness; the wealthy must love propriety." As the tycoon Li Ka-shing echoed: "Reputation is your second life; it brings business seeking you." Tony firmly believes that all financial defaults are, at their core, defaults of integrity and honor.

By anchoring his enterprise to this immovable anchor of credibility, Tony transformed raw market competition into mutual flourishing. It was this exact reputation that commanded the trust of global investment titans, securing a historic $4 million backing from legendary investor Oei Hong Leong and a $500,000 angel investment from Steve Lau (Fortune Telecom), propelling his enterprise, Pacificnet, to its milestone Initial Public Offering on the U.S. NASDAQ stock exchange—cementing a baseline of enduring, sustainable success.

V. Virtue Anchors Enterprise; Trust Fields Abundance

From the primordial glory of Emperor Yao and the historical legacy of the Greater Bay Area in Tangjiawan to the modern closing bell of the Nasdaq technology exchange, Tony Tong has written his life story into a field manual for the next generation of global builders. He inherited the unbreakable fighting spirit of his father's name and seamlessly fused the ideals of humanity and morality into his own stature. He embodies the majestic aura of our heritage and sets a standard for the modern Confucian Merchant (儒商) and Scholar-General (儒将).

The grand stature of Tang is fully realized in his conduct. He is the pride of our family, and his journey shines brightly as a living, enduring testament to the timeless path of RuDao.

Inscribed by his father, Tong Cho Sam 唐楚三
October 6, 2026

大唐威仪 唐家骄傲 《儒道》后记
--唐氏传奇-父亲的骄傲

唐仪(TonyTong.cc)-大唐威仪:先贤云:"名不正，则言不顺；言不顺，则事不成。"回望他半生行止，其名中的"仪"字，恰恰成了他大江大河人生路上的精神锚点，更是大唐威仪与唐氏家族的骄傲。

一、圣贤源流，盛唐气象

唐氏家族自古便流淌着大智慧与治国安邦的战略基因。追溯华夏文明的破晓曙光，唐氏源流可直抵上古时期的尧舜盛世。上古圣王帝尧最初受封于唐地，开创了被后世万代万民歌颂的"陶唐氏"时代，其子孙后裔遂以国为氏，这便是中国最古老、最尊贵的姓氏之一。从上古先贤"唐尧"的禅让仁政，到战国时期纵横家张仪凭大谋不战分化强敌、奠定乾坤，这种兼济天下的血脉图腾，早已深深烙印在唐门文脉之中。

及至隋唐，盛唐气象，威仪天下。唐朝不仅是鼎盛的象征，更是文化包容与世界大同的典范。大唐之威，不在于穷兵黩武，而在于万邦来朝的盛世礼乐与兼济天下的王道胸怀。

回望近代，中华民国首任内阁总理唐绍仪便诞生于 1862 年的南中国海畔——珠海唐家湾。这里正是如今粤港澳大湾区(Greater Bay Area)的核心腹地，亦是中国当代经济腾飞与科技崛起的前沿发起地。古镇中巍然屹立的"唐家三庙"，无言地见证了唐氏宗族"君子富，好行其德"的浩大声势，更承载了岭南唐门代代相传的治世大略与宗族威仪。当年唐绍仪公在任中山模范县长时，将私家园林"共乐园"慷慨赠予家乡公用——这种不图私利、造福桑梓、主张王道共赢的举措，便是最纯正的"仁者"风范与"义利共存"的祖传垂范。作为唐家子孙，唐仪天生便流淌着这种兼收并蓄、谋略远大的血脉基因与地缘灵气。

二、楚虽三户，唐门铁血

家中有严父，方能育英才。我的名字叫做唐楚三，这个名字里寄托着华夏历史深处最刚烈、最不屈的精神脊梁——"楚虽三户，亡秦必楚"。

这句震撼千古的雄心壮志，源自大汉历史学家司马迁所著的《史记·项羽本纪》。其历史典故出自战国末期楚国的南公，当时面对强秦压境、山河破碎，南公立下断言，预言了属于华族儿女保家卫国、抵御外侮、坚韧不拔的铁血奋斗精神(Fighting Spirit)。纵使身处绝境，哪怕楚国只剩下最后三户人家，也绝不向侵略者低头，其坚贞不屈的复国斗志也终将战胜强权！

这种刻在唐门骨子里的不屈斗志，不仅伴随了我一生作为机电工程师、发明家在时代的惊涛骇浪中投身国家建设，更在潜移默化中化作了唐仪永不服输的基因。正是有了这种敢于直面风雨、抵御外部危机、坚守寸土不让的唐门铁血精神，他在跨国创业、波谲云诡的全球科技赛道上，才能拥有那份"临危不惧、绝处逢生"的硬核韧性。

三、礼乐交融，文脉浸润

在这种文武兼备、礼乐相济的家风熏陶下，唐仪成长的每一步都彰显着华族君子之"仪"。他自幼受琴棋书画滋养，五岁启蒙，九岁时便能作为学校的唯一代表，在全市文艺会演中登台进行小提琴独奏。步入高中，他荣幸地考入了香港顶尖的学府——英皇书院(King's College)，并入选香港青少年管弦乐团。大学时期，他负笈远渡，考入美国明尼苏达大学(University of Minnesota)深造。

这一路走来，礼乐交融，文脉浸润。琴棋书画不仅磨砺了他的战略格局，更赋予了他内圣外王、德才兼备的仁者底色。

四、名中藏道，礼仪化义

在中文里，"仪"与"义"同音同源，其核心更在于演化为华夏治世的"礼仪"大纲。

追溯文字的本源，繁体字"儀"的甲骨文与金文结构，左边是立地顶天的"人"，右边则是"义(義)"。右侧的"义"最初是一把威武的兵器，代表着一位头戴盛饰、手持戈戟、捍卫公道的威武勇士。这位勇士在神圣的法度中展现出的规矩、风范与至高姿态，便是"仪"的文字本源。

而"仪"自古与"礼"密不可分。"礼(禮)"字的甲骨文字形，其上为珍贵的玉石，其下为东周时期的神圣祭祀青铜器皿(豆)。它的本义是双手奉上盛满玉帛的圣器，向天地神明与列祖列宗表达最高敬畏的祭祀仪式。在华夏文明中，"礼"是天地人神运行的至高法则，代表着社会制度的秩序、尊卑有度与契约共识；而"仪"则是"礼"在人世间具体展现的优雅法度与具体法门。有礼无仪则流于空洞，有仪无礼则流于虚浮。礼仪合一，便构成了中国人立身处世、治国安邦、规范天下的最高信任体制。

"礼仪"者，文明之秩序、威仪天下；"义"者，宜也，裁决道义也。而在三十年跨界创业、横跨四波高科技浪潮的现代商海里，唐仪将这股源自"礼、仪、义"三字交织的顶级华族智慧，淬炼为属于现代儒商的时代大"义"——义利共存，和而不同。

世人皆言商场如沙场，崇尚零和博弈、你死我活；唐仪却以一柄由"礼仪字源"淬炼出的"信义"长剑，破开冰冷的算法时代。在 Web3、AI 与跨国金融交织的今天，他用亲自实践证明:真正的君子与仁者，从不以牺牲情义去换取短期的浮利，而是以"礼仪"规范商业契约，以"信义"筑就长远同盟。孔子曰"见利思义，富而好礼"，李嘉诚云"信誉人格，生意来求你"。唐仪坚信，所有的逾期本质上都是信义的逾期。

因为他重情义、守信用，将信誉视作第二生命，方能在残酷商战中化博弈为双赢联盟。正是凭借这种"以德为略、以信为基"的伟岸人格与字源真谛，他的 Pacificnet 公司才能够赢得投资巨擘黄鸿年先生(注资 400 万美元)与天使投资人刘小鹰先生(注资 50 万美元)风云际会、倾力相助，最终成功于美国纳斯达克挂牌上市，筑就基业长青的稳健大局。

五、德是商之本，信是利之源

从上古陶唐的始祖荣光、珠海唐家湾的大湾区血脉，到跨国科技前沿的纳斯达克钟声，唐仪用前半生的精彩跨界与不凡成就，将自己的名字写成了华族后辈的一份行动指南。他继承了父亲名中那份百折不挠的铁血斗志，又将"人"与"义"完美融于自身的"仪"字之中，诠释了大唐式的巍峨威仪，垂范了一位现代"儒商"与"儒将"的至高境界。

大唐威仪，实至名归。此乃唐家的骄傲，亦是历久弥新的《儒道》。

父亲:唐楚三 Tong Cho Sam 谨识

V.

RuDao in Pictures · 书影

《儒道:超越孙子兵法》— photo gallery of the book, its launch events, and the classics that inspired it.

VI.

The Five Pillars

五常 · The Five Constants at the heart of RuDao

仁

Ren · Benevolence

Treat partners, employees and competitors as people, not positions. Profit earned with kindness compounds.

义

Yi · Righteousness

The courage to walk away from the deal that should never be made. Reputation is built in the deals you refuse.

礼

Li · Propriety

Form, ritual, the right words at the right time. In business, this is the architecture of trust.

智

Zhi · Wisdom

Knowing what to do, and — harder — what not to do. The strategic restraint of the sage.

信

Xin · Trust

The currency that outlasts every other. Enduring partnerships are built on the trust you cannot buy back.

VII.

Three Principles of Co-opetition

三大原则 — The operational core of RuDao, distilled from sixteen chapters into three disciplines.

01
义利共存

Profit & Trust Coexist

Profit is not the enemy of principle — greed is. When 义 (righteousness) and 利 (profit) are aligned, each reinforces the other. The Confucian merchant does not sacrifice margin for morals; he designs deals where the moral path is also the profitable one.

利润与信任共存:义利合一是儒商的根基。

02
竞合并行

Competition & Cooperation in Parallel

Compete where the market is real; cooperate where the value is shared. The modern enterprise is not a zero-sum arena but a layered ecosystem. RuDao teaches the strategic intelligence to know which layer you are in — and which mode to deploy.

竞争与合作并行:在竞争中寻求合作，在合作中保持竞争力。

03
共赢联盟

Win-Win Alliances

The goal is not to defeat the rival but to grow the pie together. Enduring partnerships — between companies, across borders, between founders and investors — are built when both sides define success as mutual flourishing, not unilateral victory.

共赢联盟:不是击败对手，而是共同做大蛋糕。

VIII.

Beyond The Art of War

孫子兵法 vs 儒道 — Not a rejection, but an evolution. The same world, a wider lens.

Dimension The Art of War
孫子兵法		 RuDao
儒道
Core Logic Zero-sum game
零和博弈		 Positive-sum game
正和博弈
Relationship to Rival Defeat the enemy
击败敌人		 Make the rival a partner
化敌为友
Time Horizon Short-term victory
短期胜利		 Sustainable, enduring partnerships
可持续长远关系
Strategic Posture Deception & maneuver
谋略与欺骗		 Trust & transparency
信任与透明
View of Profit Maximize own gain
最大化己方利益		 Balanced profit, shared value
平衡利润，共享价值
Ethical Frame Utility, not morality
功利，不论道德		 Virtue as strategy
以德为略
Ultimate Goal Win the war
赢得战争		 Build the alliance
建立联盟

RuDao does not discard Sun Tzu. It absorbs him. The Art of War remains the sharpest tool for the zero-sum layer — the battle you must win. But RuDao asks: must every encounter be a battle? The answer, confirmed by three decades of cross-border commerce, is no. The greater game is the one where the rival becomes a partner and the partnership outlasts the deal.

IX.

The Lineage

儒商 · 儒将 · 天人合一 — Three traditions, one way

RuDao does not invent a philosophy. It braids three strands of Chinese thought into a single cord strong enough for modern commerce — the virtue of the scholar-merchant, the strategy of the scholar-general, and the Taoist insight that heaven and humanity are one.

儒商
The Business Ideal

Confucian Merchant

Commerce conducted with integrity, not despite it. The 儒商 trades in trust as much as in goods — believing that a deal well made serves both counterparties, and that reputation is the only balance sheet that never needs auditing.

儒将
The Strategic Ideal

Confucian General

Power tempered by learning, force guided by virtue. The 儒将 wins without burning the field he intends to harvest — competing fiercely where stakes are real, but never mistaking a rival for an enemy.

天人合一
The Philosophical Root

Unity of Heaven & Humanity

From Taoism: the conviction that 天人合一 — heaven, earth and people form a single living system. Business is not separate from life; profit is not separate from people. Enduring success flows only from alignment with this whole.

核心语录 · Key Teachings

和而不同，共创长远。

Harmony in difference; co-create the long horizon.

义利合一，信立商道。

Righteousness and profit unified; trust establishes the merchant's way.

竞合并行，化敌为友。

Compete and cooperate in parallel; turn rivals into friends.

以德为略，以信为基。

Virtue as strategy; trust as foundation.

天人合一，商道即人道。

Heaven and humanity are one; the way of business is the way of being human.

匠人之心，共赢联盟。

The craftsman's heart; the win-win alliance.

The Author

TonyTong.cc 唐仪

儒道 · 儒商 · 儒将 ·

儒商 · 儒将 · Cross-border Entrepreneur · Investor · HKBA Co-Founder · Adjunct Professor · US Patent Holder

Tony Tong (唐仪) is a global entrepreneur, seasoned cross-border investor, and business strategist. He is the co-founder of the Hong Kong Blockchain Association (HKBA) — Hong Kong's leading industry body for blockchain, fintech and Web3 — and an adjunct professor at the International Technological University (ITU.edu), the University of Minnesota, PolyU, and the Hong Kong Financial Services Institute (HKFSI).

With decades of experience spanning international commerce, fintech, cross-border negotiation, and global alliance building, he has witnessed countless zero-sum business conflicts, short-term victories, and broken partnerships across global markets. He holds US Patent No. 6,012,066 for an Internet workflow system and is a frequent event speaker at UBS, Goldman Sachs, Morgan Stanley, JPMorgan Chase, Bank of America Merrill Lynch, CLSA, Jeffries, Roth Capital, G2E, GITEX, Consensus, Korea Blockchain Week, Vietnam Blockchain Association, Economist, and Forbes.

Tony integrates Confucian ethics with modern global business strategy. Moving beyond the purely competitive mindset of The Art of War, he created the 儒将竞合战略 (Ru-Jiang co-opetition framework) — a balanced strategic system that combines fierce competition with sincere cooperation, profit pursuit with long-term trust, and individual success with enduring partnership value.

He writes as a 儒商 — a Confucian merchant who believes commerce and conscience are not opposites — and as a student of the 儒将 tradition, the scholar-general who wields strategy without surrendering virtue. RuDao draws these lineages together with the Taoist insight of 天人合一 — the unity of heaven and humanity — to offer a way of business that is balanced, enduring, and whole.

RuDao is the book he wished he had when he started.

Professor Kuang-Shih Yeh 葉匡時教授

Vice Mayor of Kaohsiung City 2018–2020, Taiwan

China has a long and profound commercial tradition. The Shang Dynasty, founded over three thousand years ago, was the earliest Chinese dynasty with written records. The word "Shang" itself stands for business, commerce, and merchants. It is said that the ancestors of the Shang people were highly adept at business and trade, which is how the dynasty came to be named "Shang". Over two thousand years ago, the Records of the Grand Historian (Shiji史记)—the first book to systematically document Chinese history—included a dedicated chapter titled "Biographies of the Money-makers" (Huo Zhi Lie Chuan). Its core content focused on the path to wealth and the management wisdom of prominent historical merchants. On the other hand, throughout successive dynasties, Confucianism and Taoism—the two most influential schools of thought shaping Chinese culture—have placed immense value on fundamental human principles and the philosophy of life. Chapter Two of this book, titled "The Eight Virtues," explores the essential ethical values familiar to every Chinese person. In traditional Chinese values, a respected and successful entrepreneur is honored as a "Confucian Entrepreneurs" (Ru Shang). A Confucian Entrepreneurs is not merely someone skilled at making money, but rather an individual who firmly upholds traditional Confucian core values while managing a business. I am delighted to see the author explain this concept of the "Confucian Entrepreneurs" so clearly. The author, Tony Tong, has received a complete education in both Western and Chinese traditions, possesses a deep understanding of cutting-edge technology, and brings a wealth of successful business experience. It can truly be said that he is a respected Confucian Entrepreneur himself. By integrating the fundamental values and wisdom of Confucianism and Taoism into the modern business world, he emphasizes "co-opetition" rather than "zero-sum," proving that traditional Chinese wisdom remains fully applicable to today's complex commercial landscape. In an era marked by rapid technological advancements, complex dynamic environments, and geopolitical volatility, a guiding North Star is increasingly vital for business leaders. I am deeply grateful for Tony Tong's contribution in clearly articulating the wisdom of Confucian Entrepreneurs in this book. These insights serve as the North Star guiding us forward, making this a work well worth studying, whether or not you are actively engaged in the business world.

中國有悠久深厚的商業傳統。立國於三千多年前的商朝，是中國最早有文字的朝代。而「商」就是代表商業、商人，據說，商朝人的祖先很會做生意，經營商業，故以「商」為名。兩千多年前，第一本完整記錄中國歷史的書籍《史記》有一專篇「貨殖列傳」，其內容重點就是歷史上知名商人的致富之道與經營智慧。

另一方面，歷朝歷代以來，影響中國思想與文化最重要的儒家與道家，非常重視做人的基本價值與處世之道，《儒道 RuDao 》這本書的第二章「儒商八德」談的就是每個中國人都熟悉的基本倫理價值。在中國傳統的價值觀裡，受人尊敬的成功商人被稱之為「儒商」，儒商未必是很會賺錢的商人，而是能在經營事業時，仍能堅守傳統儒家的基本價值。我很高興見到作者把這個「儒商」的觀念，講得這麼清楚。

作者唐儀TonyTong 受過完整的中西方教育，理解前沿科技，且具有豐富成功的商業經驗，可以說，他就是一位受尊重的儒商。他把儒家與道家的基本價值與智慧融入現代商場上，強調要「競和」而非「零和」，證明傳統中國智慧仍能適用於現代的複雜商業社會。面對科技迅猛發展、環境複雜多變、地緣政治動盪不安的新時代，引領企業經營者前進的北極星日益重要。感謝唐儀的貢獻，在這本書清楚地闡揚中國的儒商智慧，而這些儒商智慧就是引導我們前進的北極星，不論你是否是在商場上馳騁，這本書都值得我們研讀。

— 葉匡時教授 Professor Kuang-Shih Yeh - 台灣大學政治系學士、美國德拉瓦大學公共行政碩士以及美國卡內基梅隆大學組織理論博士。現任富邦金控以及公信電子獨立董事、蘋信科技以及上海悅心健康集團董事、中華大學講座教授、陽明山未來學社榮譽理事長以及亞太公私合夥建設發展協會榮譽理事長。 曾任中山大學企管系教授兼主任、傳播管理研究所所長、醫務管理研究所所長以及 EMBA 執行長，政治大學科技管理所教授，北京清華大學公共管理學院客座教授，上海復旦大學管理學院講座教授等職。在馬英九政府時期，先後擔任過行政院研考會副主委、交通部政務次長、部長，以及桃園機場公司首任董事長，後來也曾擔任過高雄市副市長。曾任台灣銀行、聯強國際等多家公司獨立董事、太平洋聯網科技營運長、實聯精密化學副董事長等職務。著有《總經理的新衣》、《總經理的內衣》等六本管理專書，並三度榮獲經濟部中小企業處金書獎。

Dr Jiaming Zhu 朱嘉明教授

Economist · 2nd October 2026

Long-term business success depends on balancing profit and moral responsibility, competition and cooperation, strategy and trust. In RuDao, Tony Tong brings Confucian thought together with his experience in cross-border business, offering a framework for corporate decision-making and partnerships that combines ethics with competitiveness.

Beginning with the principle of generating profit through righteousness and the virtues of the Confucian entrepreneur, the book explores strategic choices in negotiations, alliances and crises, linking inner moral cultivation with leadership in the wider world. The book's discussion of AI governance, Web3 and the token economy places traditional thought in a new technological context. It invites readers to consider how benevolence, righteousness, propriety, wisdom and trustworthiness can become practical business principles, enabling the pursuit of profit alongside the development of enduring partnerships.

商业的长期发展，需要处理好利润与道义、竞争与合作、策略与信任的关系。唐仪的《儒道》将儒家思想与自身跨境商业实践相结合，为企业决策与伙伴关系提供兼顾伦理和竞争力的思考框架。

本书从"义以生利"与儒商德性出发，讨论谈判、同盟和危机中的战略选择，并将"内圣"与"外王"联系起来。书中对人工智能治理、Web3与Token经济的讨论，将传统思想置于新的技术环境之中，启发读者思考:如何把仁、义、礼、智、信转化为经营原则，在追求利润的同时，建立持久的合作关系。

— Dr Jiaming Zhu - 2nd October 2026 朱嘉明 2026.10.2

Yam Tak Cheung 任德章 MH

Chairman, Integrated Capital 本滙融資主席 · 福布斯的中国买家 Forbes Buyer from Hong Kong · 3rd October 2026

In investing, the greatest returns come from time, and time rewards those who can be trusted. In RuDao, Tony Tong begins from the Confucian principle of generating profit through righteousness, and shows that integrity is not a cost of doing business but an asset that compounds and never depreciates.

The book does not reject The Art of War; it moves beyond it. Competition need not be zero-sum, and today's rival can become tomorrow's partner. Drawing on decades of cross-border experience and the Confucian ideal of uniting inner cultivation with outward leadership, Tony offers entrepreneurs, investors and managers a practical framework for long-term thinking. At a time of rapid geopolitical and technological change, this wisdom of building on trust and profiting through righteousness deserves careful reading.

在資本市場，真正的回報往往來自時間，而時間只會站在值得信任的人一邊。唐儀的《儒道》以「義以生利」為起點，說明誠信並非經營的成本，而是一項可以持續累積、永不折舊的資產。

本書並不否定《孫子兵法》，而是在其基礎上更進一步:競爭不必是零和，今天的對手亦可成為明天的夥伴。作者結合數十年跨境經營的經驗，以「內聖外王」貫通德性與策略，為企業家、投資者與管理者提供一套可實踐的長期主義思考。在地緣格局與科技急速變化的今天，這種以信立業、以義取利的智慧，尤其值得細讀。

— Yam Tak Cheung, MH · Chairman, Integrated Capital 任德章 MH 本滙融資主席 · 3rd October 2026 2026年10月3日
